Human validation packet 3 (v2, annotator 2), part B: what does a solution state? (600 items, blinded)

Work through the items in the order given, and do part B before part A.

Each item shows a math word problem and one solution sampled from a language model (at most its last 1,800 characters, so it may begin mid-sentence). Judge what the text states, not whether it is correct, and do not assume that a problem is, or is not, fully specified. The same problem appears several times with different solutions; judge each item on its own. Do not use any AI assistance, and do not discuss the items with anyone.

For each item, record two labels in your own blank copy of the template (columns hid,flag,answer,note):

  • flag: Y if the visible text explicitly says that information is missing, ambiguous, contradictory or unrealistic, or explicitly states an assumption it had to make in order to proceed (e.g. "assuming ...", "the problem does not say ..., so ..."); N otherwise, including when it silently uses a value, only defines a variable for a quantity the problem determines, or restates the problem.
  • answer: NUM if the solution ends with a final numeric answer to this problem; DECLINE if it declines to give one because information is missing, ambiguous or contradictory; NONE if it gives no final answer for another reason (cut off, no number, unrelated text).

B2-001

Problem. Crystal wanted to sell everything left in her pastry class before closing for the night. She reduced her $3.00 cupcakes and her $2.00 cookies by some discount. How much money did she make if she sold the last 16 cupcakes and 8 cookies?
 First, we need to determine the discounted price for the cupcakes and cookies. Since the problem doesn't specify the discount, let's calculate how much money she made with the original prices.

Step 1: Calculate the total revenue from cupcakes.
- Crystal sold 16 cupcakes at $3.00 each.
- Total revenue from cupcakes = 16 * $3.00 = $48.00

Step 2: Calculate the total revenue from cookies.
- Crystal sold 8 cookies at $2.00 each.
- Total revenue from cookies = 8 * $2.00 = $16.00

Step 3: Calculate the total revenue from both cupcakes and cookies.
- Total revenue = Total revenue from cupcakes + Total revenue from cookies
- Total revenue = $48.00 + $16.00 = $64.00

Answer: 64

B2-002

Problem. Faye had 46 math problems and 9 science problems for homework. If she finished 40 of the problems at school, how many science problems did she have to do for homework?
 First, we need to determine the total number of problems Faye had initially:
  
  Total problems = Math problems + Science problems
                 = 46 + 9
                 = 55

Next, we subtract the number of problems she finished at school from the total number of problems to find out how many problems she has left:
  
  Problems left = Total problems - Finished problems
                = 55 - 40
                = 15
  
So, Faye has 15 problems left to do for homework. Since we don't know the proportion of math and science problems between the ones left to do, we'll assume that she still has a ratio of 46 math problems to 9 science problems, which simplifies to approximately 5.11 math problems per science problem.

To approximate how many of the remaining 15 problems are science problems, we use the simplified ratio:

  Approximate science problems left = Remaining problems / (Math problems ratio + Science problems)
                                     = 15 / (5.11 + 1)
                                     = 15 / 6.11
                                     ≈ 2.46

Since the number of problems must be an integer, we round this number to the nearest whole number:

  Final number of science problems left ≈ 2

Answer: 2

B2-003

Problem. A painter needed to paint 6 rooms in a building. Each room takes several hours to paint. If he already painted 2 rooms, how much longer will he take to paint the rest?
 Paint the total number of rooms: 6 rooms / day * 3 days / room = 2 days. Find the number of hours for one room: 2 days * 8 hours / day = 16 hours. Calculate the time it took for the first two rooms: 16 hours / room * 2 rooms = 32 hours. Subtract the painted rooms from the total number of rooms: 6 rooms - 2 rooms = 4 rooms. Calculate the number of hours for the other rooms: 16 hours / room * 4 rooms = 64 hours. Answer: 64.

B2-004

Problem. The cost of filling a car fuel tank is $45, and this can take you 500 miles before refilling. Mallory was going to her grammy's house, 2000 miles away. If she used 3/5 times as much money on food as she spent on filling the car fuel tank for the whole journey, calculate the total amount of money she used.
 First, calculate the number of times Mallory had to fill the car fuel tank for the whole journey.

Mallory needs to drive 2000 miles, and each fill can get her 500 miles. So, the number of times she needed to fill the tank is:

Number of fills = Total miles / Miles per fill
Number of fills = 2000 miles / 500 miles
Number of fills = 4

Now, calculate the total cost of filling the car fuel tank for the whole journey.

Total fuel cost = Number of fills × Cost per fill
Total fuel cost = 4 × $45
Total fuel cost = $180

Next, calculate how much Mallory spent on food.

Mallory spent 3/5 times as much money on food as she did on filling the car fuel tank.

Food cost = (3/5) × Total fuel cost
Food cost = (3/5) × $180
Food cost = $108

Finally, calculate the total amount of money Mallory used for the whole journey.

Total money used = Total fuel cost + Food cost
Total money used = $180 + $108
Total money used = $288

Answer: 288

 The ants double each hour, so there were / were 50 / 2 = 25 ants in the jar one hour before. This is half the number that you started with, so there were 25 * 2 = 50 ants in the jar two hours before. So there are 50 - 2 = 48 ants in the jar. Answer: 48

B2-007

Problem. Kim drives 30 miles to her friend's house. On the way back she has to take a detour that is 20% longer. She spends 30 minutes at her friend's house. She drives at a speed of 44 mph. How long did she spend away from home?
 The trip there took 30 / 44 = 0.68 hours. The trip back took 30 / 44 = 0.68 hours. This round trip with the change of route was 0.68 + 0.68 = 1.36 hours. At 20% longer this detour was 30 * 1.2 = 36 miles. So it took 36 / 44 = 0.82 hours to get home on the detour. This increased the overall trip time to 0.82 + 1.36 = 2.18 hours. Plus the extra 30 minutes at her friend’s house makes this 218 minutes + 30 minutes = 248 minutes away from home. Answer: 248.

Problem. Justin and Sabrina at Pine Grove Elementary have many pencils combined. Justin has 8 more than twice as many pencils as Sabrina. How many pencils does Sabrina have?
 
Starting with the reasoning:
1. Let's use the variable 'S' to represent the number of penknives that Sabrina has. 
2. According to the information given, Justin has 8 more than twice as many pencils as Sabrina. So, Justin has '2S + 8' pencils. 
3. Let's add up the total number of pencils that Justin and Sabrina have: 'S + (2S + 8) = 3S + 8' 
4. We do not have information about the total number of pencils, but we still have an equation with the variable 'S'. Thus, we can not determine the exact number of pencils that Sabrina has based on this information.

Answer: We can not determine the exact number of pencils Sabrina has, as we need additional information such as the total number of pencils they have or the total number Justin has.

B2-009

Problem. Ed had 19 more marbles than Doug. Ed lost some of his marbles at the playground. Now Ed has 8 more marbles than doug..How many marbles did Ed lose?
 
1. Let's denote the number of marbles Doug has as D.
2. According to the information, Ed initially had 19 more marbles than Doug, so Ed had D + 19 marbles.
3. After losing some marbles, Ed now has 8 more marbles than Doug, so Ed has D + 8 marbles.
4. To find out how many marbles Ed lost, we subtract the number of marbles Ed has now from the number of marbles he had initially: (D + 19) - (D + 8).
5. Simplifying the expression, we get: D + 19 - D - 8 = 19 - 8 = 11 marbles.

Answer: 11 marbles

Problem. On an American flag, the first stripe is red and half of the remaining stripes are also red. Each flag has 13 stripes. John buys 10 flags. How many red stripes are there?
 With 13 stripes, and the first stripe already being red, there are 12 stripes left. Half of 12 is 6 so the number of remaining red stripes is 6. Including the first, there are 1 + 6 = 7 red stripes in a flag. With 10 flags, the number of red stripes is 7 * 10 = 70. Answer: 70.

She bought several boxes of flour that cost $3 each box, 3 trays of eggs that cost $10 for each tray, 7 liters of milk that cost $5 each liter, and 2 boxes of baking soda that cost $3 each box. How much will she have to pay for everything?
 The cost of the flour is 3 boxes / box * $3 / box = $9. She bought 7 liters of milk for $5 each, then she paid 7 liters / liter * $5 / liter = $35 for the milk. The 2 boxes of baking soda cost $3 per box, so they totaled 2 boxes / box * $3 / box = $6 for the baking soda. Then it means that the total price of all the buying is $9 + $10*3 + $35 + $6 = $90.

B2-014

Problem. Jo-Bob hopped into the hot air balloon, released the anchor rope, and pulled on the lift chain, which ignited the flame and provided the warm air that caused the balloon to rise. When the lift chain was pulled, the balloon would rise at a rate of 50 feet per minute. But when the chain was not being pulled, the balloon would slowly descend at a rate of dozens of feet per minute. During his balloon ride, he pulled the chain for 15 minutes, then released the rope for 10 minutes, then pulled the chain for another 15 minutes, and finally released the chain and allowed the balloon to slowly descend back to the earth. During his balloon ride, what was the highest elevation reached by the balloon?
 To find the highest elevation reached by the balloon, we need to determine the elevation after each chain-pulling interval. Since the balloon rises at a rate of 50 feet per minute while the chain is being pulled:

- After the first 15 minutes of chain-pulling: \(50 \text{ ft/min} \times 15 \text{ min} = 750 \text{ feet}\)
- After releasing the chain for 10 minutes (while descending): We don't need to calculate descent here because we're interested in the highest point. 
- After the second 15 minutes of chain-pulling: \(750 \text{ feet} + (50 \text{ ft/min} \times 15 \text{ min}) = 750 \text{ feet} + 750 \text{ feet} = 1,500 \text{ feet}\)

So, the highest elevation reached by the balloon during Jo-Bob's ride was 1,500 feet.

Answer: 1500

Problem. A convenience store sold 100 bags of chips in a month. In the first week, 15 bags of chips were sold. In the second week, thrice as many bags of chips were sold. There was an equal number of chips sold in the third and fourth week. How many chips was each sold in the third and fourth week?
 There were a total of 100 bags – 15 – 3 * 15 = 40 chips sold in the 3rd and 4th week combined. Thus, a total of 40 / 2 = 20 chips were sold each week. Answer: 20.

B2-018

Problem. There are one-third as many Ford trucks as Dodge trucks in the parking lot of the Taco Castle. But there are twice as many Ford trucks as Toyota trucks in this parking lot, and there are half as many Volkswagen Bugs as there are Toyota trucks in this same parking lot. How many Dodge trucks are in the parking lot of the Taco Castle?
 Let the total number of Dodge trucks in the parking lot of the Taco Castle be D, the total number of Ford trucks is D / 3 and the total number of Toyota trucks is (D / 3) / 2 (twice as many Ford trucks as Toyota trucks) = D / 6.The total number of Volkswagen Bugs in the parking lot of the Taco Castle is (D / 6) / 2 (half as many Volkswagen Bugs as Toyota trucks) = D / 12.We know that the only trucks in this parking lot are Dodge trucks, Ford trucks, Toyota trucks, and Volkswagen Bugs so there is a total of D + D / 3 + D / 6 + D / 12 = 33D / 12 = 11D / 4 trucks in the parking lot of the Taco Castle. 33D / 12 = 11D / 4 is the same as D / 4 = 1 (whichever truck there is only one of), so D = 4.Answer: 4.

Problem. On a particular day, Rose's teacher read the register and realized there were twice as many girls as boys present on that day. The class has 250 students, and all the 140 girls were present. If all the absent students were boys, how many boys were absent that day?
 The total number of students in the class is 250, and all 140 girls are present. The number of boys present is 250 - 140 = 110. Since there are twice as many girls as boys present, there should be 140 / 2 = 70 boys present. Thus, the number of boys absent is 110 - 70 = 40.
Answer: 40

Problem. A book has many chapters. Beth has read 2 chapters in 3 hours. If she continues reading at that rate, in how many hours will she be able to finish reading the remaining chapters?
 Beth reads 2 chapters in 3 hours, so her reading rate is 2/3 chapters per hour. Now we need to find out how many chapters are left to read and then calculate the time it will take her to finish reading them.

Let's assume there are a total of C chapters in the book and s chapters are already read (s = 2). So, the number of remaining chapters is R = C - s.

To find the time it will take her to finish reading the remaining chapters, we can use the formula:

Time = (Remaining Chapters) / (Reading Rate)

Plugging in the values, we get:

Time = (C - 2) / (2/3)

To find the final numeric answer, we need to know the total number of chapters (C) in the book. Since that information is not provided, we cannot determine the final numeric answer.

Problem. Julia has $40. She spends part of her money to buy a new game for her phone. She spends a quarter of what she has left on in-game purchases. How much money does she have left?
 Let X be the amount she spent on a new game for her phone. She has 40 - X dollars left. She spends 0.25 * (40 - X) dollars, so she has 40 - X - 4 - 0.25X = 36 - 1.25X dollars left. Since we don't know how much she spent on a new game for her phone, we can't simplify any further. To maximize the amount of money she has left, we can assume she didn't spend any money on a new game for her phone, so X = 0. Her final amount of money will be 36 - 1.25 * 0 = 36 dollars. The final answer: 36 dollars.

Problem. A large bag of balls was kept under Haley's bed. Her mom placed the balls in bags for children in foster homes. If every bag can contain 4 balls and Haley has hundreds of balls. How many bags will be used?
 We need to find out how many bags are required to put all the balls. The information given tells us that each bag can hold 4 balls and Haley has hundreds of balls. 

To determine the number of bags needed, we need to divide the total number of balls by the capacity of each bag. Since we don't know the exact number of balls, we can use a variable (let's say "x") to represent the total number of balls.

So, the number of bags needed will be: x / 4

Since we know there are hundreds of balls, we can assume that "x" is a multiple of 4. Let's say Haley has 100 balls (the minimum number of hundreds). In this case, the number of bags needed would be: 100 / 4 = 25 bags.

But since Haley has hundreds of balls, we need to consider a general case for any multiple of 4 (up to one hundred or more). So, the number of bags needed will always be x / 4, where x is a multiple of 4.

Answer: The number of bags needed will be x / 4, where x is a multiple of 4.

B2-035

Problem. Amalia can read many pages of her book in 2 minutes. How many minutes will it take her to read 18 pages of her book?
 We need to find out how long it takes Amalia to read one page. Since she can read many pages in 2 minutes, we can assume she reads at a constant rate.

Step 1: Determine the reading rate in pages per minute.
Since Amalia can read many pages in 2 minutes, we need to find out the rate at which she reads. Let's say she reads X pages in 2 minutes. Therefore, her reading rate is X pages / 2 minutes.

Step 2: Calculate the time it takes to read 18 pages.
Now we want to find out how long it takes her to read 18 pages. To do this, we can set up a proportion using her reading rate:

X pages / 2 minutes = 18 pages / t minutes

Step 3: Solve for t.
We can cross-multiply to solve for t:

X * t = 18 * 2
t = (18 * 2) / X

Problem. The sum of three consecutive even numbers is 246..What are the numbers?

According to the problem, their sum is 246. So, we can write the equation:

x + (x + 2) + (x + 4) = 246

Now let's solve for x:

3x + 6 = 246
3x = 240
x = 80

Now we have the three consecutive even numbers:

First number: x = 80
Second number: x + 2 = 82
Third number: x + 4 = 84

Answer: 80, 82, 84

B2-074

Problem. Hadley loves to do volunteer work at the local library. On a certain day, she neatly arranges 100 books on the shelf. By lunchtime, a certain number of books had been borrowed. She added 40 more books to the same shelf. By evening, 30 more books had been borrowed. If the shelf had 60 books remaining by the evening, how many books had been borrowed by lunchtime?
 Let \( x \) be the number of books borrowed by lunchtime. Initially, there are 100 books. By lunchtime, \( x \) books are borrowed, leaving \( 100 - x \) books on the shelf. Hadley then adds 40 more books, so the number of books on the shelf becomes \( 100 - x + 40 = 140 - x \). By the evening, 30 more books are borrowed, so the number of books remaining on the shelf is \( 140 - x - 30 = 110 - x \). We are given that there are 60 books remaining by the evening, so we can set up the equation \( 110 - x = 60 \). Solving for \( x \), we get:

\[
110 - x = 60
\]

Subtract 60 from both sides:

\[
50 = x
\]

Therefore, the number of books borrowed by lunchtime is \( \boxed{50} \).

B2-081

Problem. 23 children were riding on the bus. At the bus stop 24 children got on the bus while some got off the bus. Then there were 8 children altogether on the bus..How many more children got off the bus than those that got on?
 To find out how many more children got off the bus than those that got on, we need to determine the number of children that got off the bus and compare it with the number of children that got on the bus.

First, let's find out the number of children that got off the bus. We know that there were originally 23 children on the bus, and after some got on and some got off, there were 8 children on the bus. So, the difference between the number of children before and after the bus stop must account for the children that got on and those that got off.

The number of children that got on the bus is 24, and the number of children that were on the bus after the bus stop is 8. Therefore, the total number of children that got off the bus is the sum of the original number of children on the bus and the number of children that got on, minus the number of children that were on the bus after the bus stop:

\[ \text{Number of children that got off} = 23 + 24 - 8 \]

Now, let's calculate this:

\[ 23 + 24 = 47 \]
\[ 47 - 8 = 39 \]

So, 39 children got off the bus.

Next, we need to find out how many more children got off the bus than those that got on. We do this by subtracting the number of children that got on from the number of children that got off:

\[ \text{Difference} = 39 - 24 \]

Now, let's calculate this:

\[ 39 - 24 = 15 \]

Therefore, 15 more children got off the bus than those that got on.

Answer: 15

Thus, Jerry has 3G = 3(0) = 0 stickers. Answer: 0.

B2-086

Problem. Marco owns an ice cream truck. His ice cream cones are $5 each. If his expenses are 80% of total sales for the day, how many ice cream cones would he need to sell to make a $200 profit for the day?
 First, we need to determine what percentage of Marco's sales are left after expenses. Since expenses are 80% of total sales, the remaining percentage for profit is 100% - 80% = 20%.

Marco wants to make a $200 profit. To find out how much total sales need to be to achieve this profit, we divide the desired profit by the percentage of sales that goes towards profit:

Total sales needed = Desired profit / Percentage for profit
Total sales needed = $200 / 0.20
Total sales needed = $1000

Since each ice cream cone is sold for $5, we need to figure out how many cones Marco needs to sell to make $1000 in total sales:

Number of cones to sell = Total sales needed / Price per cone
Number of cones to sell = $1000 / $5
Number of cones to sell = 200

So, Marco would need to sell 200 ice cream cones to make a $200 profit for the day.

Answer: 200

B2-096

Problem. Payton leaves to go on an 85 minute bike ride at 3:27 p.m. Payton's family eats dinner at 5:45 p.m..How much time will Payton have between finishing her bike ride and eating dinner?
 To find out how much time Payton will have between finishing her bike ride and eating dinner, we need to follow these steps:

1. **Determine the end time of the bike ride**: Payton starts her bike ride at 3:27 p.m. and rides for 85 minutes.

   First, we need to add 85 minutes to 3:27 p.m.
   
   - 85 minutes can be broken down into 60 minutes plus 25 minutes.
   - Adding 60 minutes to 3:27 p.m. takes us to 4:27 p.m. (since 3:27 p.m. + 60 minutes = 4:27 p.m.).
   - Adding the remaining 25 minutes to 4:27 p.m. gives us 4:52 p.m. (since 4:27 p.m. + 25 minutes = 4:52 p.m.).

   So, Payton finishes her bike ride at 4:52 p.m.

2. **Calculate the time between the end of the bike ride and dinner**: Dinner is at 5:45 p.m. We need to find the difference between 5:45 p.m. and 4:52 p.m.

   - The difference in hours is 0 (since 4:52 p.m. is earlier than 5:45 p.m.).
   - We need to find the difference in minutes: 45 minutes (from 4:52 p.m. to 5:45 p.m.) - 52 minutes.

   Since 45 minutes is less than 52 minutes, we need to borrow 1 hour (which is 60 minutes):

   - 45 minutes + 60 minutes = 105 minutes.
   - Now subtract 52 minutes from 105 minutes: 105 minutes - 52 minutes = 53 minutes.

   Therefore, the time between finishing her bike ride and eating dinner is 53 minutes.

Answer: 53

Problem. Charley has a bag that contains 51 white beads and 90 black beads. He reaches in the bag and pulls out a third of the beads. How many black beads in total did Charley pull out?
 First, we need to find the total number of beads in the bag. So, we add the white beads and black beads:

Total beads = White beads + Black beads
Total beads = 51 + 90
Total beads = 141

Now, Charley is pulling out a third of the beads. To find this, we multiply the total number of beads by 1/3:

Beads pulled out = Total beads * 1/3
Beads pulled out = 141 * 1/3
Beads pulled out = 47

Since the ratio of white to black beads is 51:90, we can find the ratio of black beads pulled out by the same ratio:

Black beads pulled out = (Total beads pulled out * Ratio of black beads) / Total ratio
Black beads pulled out = (47 * 90) / (51 + 90)
Black beads pulled out = (47 * 90) / 141
Black beads pulled out = 4230 / 141
Black beads pulled out = 30

Answer: 30

B2-106

Problem. Jo-Bob hopped into the hot air balloon, released the anchor rope, and pulled on the lift chain, which ignited the flame and provided the warm air that caused the balloon to rise. When the lift chain was pulled, the balloon would rise at a rate of 50 feet per minute. But when the chain was not being pulled, the balloon would slowly descend at a rate of dozens of feet per minute. During his balloon ride, he pulled the chain for 15 minutes, then released the rope for 10 minutes, then pulled the chain for another 15 minutes, and finally released the chain and allowed the balloon to slowly descend back to the earth. During his balloon ride, what was the highest elevation reached by the balloon?
 To find the highest elevation reached by the balloon, we need to calculate the elevation gained by pulling the chain and the elevation lost by releasing the chain.

1. When the chain was pulled for the first 15 minutes, the balloon rose at a rate of 50 feet per minute. So, in 15 minutes, it would have risen by:
   Elevation gained = Rate × Time = 50 feet/minute × 15 minutes = 750 feet

2. Next, the chain was released for 10 minutes, causing the balloon to descend. Since the rate of descent is not given, we can assume that it's less than or equal to 50 feet per minute, as mentioned in the problem. But we don't need this information for the highest elevation because the balloon reached its highest point when the chain was last pulled.

3. The chain was pulled again for another 15 minutes, so the balloon rose an additional:
   Elevation gained = Rate × Time = 50 feet/minute × 15 minutes = 750 feet

Now, we can calculate the highest elevation reached by the balloon by adding the elevation gained during the first two pulling periods:
Highest elevation = Elevation gained (first pull) + Elevation gained (second pull) = 750 feet + 750 feet = 1500 feet

Answer: 1500

B2-111

Problem. Mary is counting the number of minnows in the pond. 40% of the minnows have red bellies, 30% have green bellies and the rest have white or black bellies. If 20 minnows have red bellies, how many minnows have black bellies?
 First, let's find the total number of minnows. Since 40% of the minnows have red bellies and there are 20 minnows with red bellies, we can set up the equation 0.40 * Total minnows = 20. Solving for Total minnows, we get Total minnows = 20 / 0.40 = 50. Now, we know there are 50 minnows in total.

Next, let's find the number of minnows with green bellies. 30% of the minnows have green bellies, so 0.30 * 50 = 15 minnows have green bellies.

Now, let's find the number of minnows with red and green bellies combined: 20 (red) + 15 (green) = 35 minnows.

Finally, let's find the number of minnows with black or white bellies: 50 (total) - 35 (red and green) = 15 minnows.

Since we know that the rest have white or black bellies and we're not given any information that would allow us to differentiate between white and black, we can assume that all 15 minnows have black bellies.

Answer: 15

Problem. 9 years from now, John will be -3 times as old as he was 11 years ago. How old is he now?
 Let's denote John's current age as J. In 9 years, John's age will be J + 9. Eleven years ago, John's age was J - 11.

According to the problem, in 9 years, John will be -3 times as old as he was 11 years ago. So we can write the equation:

J + 9 = -3 * (J - 11)

Now let's solve for J:

J + 9 = -3J + 33

Combining the J terms on one side and the constants on the other side, we get:

4J = 24

Divide both sides by 4 to find J:

J = 6

So, John is currently 6 years old.

Answer: 6

Problem. The cost of filling a car fuel tank is $45, and this can take you 500 miles before refilling. Mallory was going to her grammy's house, 2000 miles away. If she used 3/5 times as much money on food as she spent on filling the car fuel tank for the whole journey, calculate the total amount of money she used.
 To calculate the total amount of money Mallory used, we need to determine the number of times she needs to refill the car's fuel tank for the 2000-mile journey and then add the cost of food.

First, let's find out how many times Mallory needs to refill her car's fuel tank for the 2000-mile journey:

2000 miles / 500 miles per tank refill = 4 tank refills

Now, let's calculate the cost of filling the car fuel tank 4 times:

4 tank refills * $45 per tank refill = $180

Next, we need to calculate how much money Mallory spent on food, which is 3/5 times the cost of filling the car fuel tank for the entire journey:

$180 * (3/5) = $108

Finally, let's add the cost of filling the car fuel tank and the cost of food to find the total amount of money Mallory used:

$180 + $108 = $288

Answer: 288

2. The difference in the scores between Hajar and Farah is 21 points.
3. Since Farah's score is higher than Hajar's, we need to add the difference to Hajar's score to find Farah's score.
4. Therefore, Farah's score = Hajar's score + difference = 24 + 21 = 45 points.
5. Now, to find the sum of their scores, we simply add Hajar's score and Farah's score together.
6. Sum of their scores = Hajar's score + Farah's score = 24 + 45 = 69 points.
Answer: 69

1. From 3:27 p.m. to 4:27 p.m., that’s 1 hour.
2. From 4:27 p.m. to 5:45 p.m., that’s 1 hour and 18 minutes.

So, the total time from 3:27 p.m. to 5:45 p.m. is 1 + 1 = 2 hours, and 0 + 18 = 18 minutes.

Now, Payton's bike ride is 85 minutes long. We need to find out how much time is left after the bike ride for dinner time, which is 2 hours and 18 minutes.

2 hours * 60 minutes/hour = 120 minutes
So, 2 hours and 18 minutes is 120 + 18 = 138 minutes.

Now subtract the duration of the bike ride from the total time:

138 minutes (time to dinner) - 85 minutes (bike ride) = 53 minutes.

Answer: 53

B2-188

Problem. The sum of three consecutive even numbers is 246..What are the numbers?
 Let x be the first even number. Then the next two consecutive even numbers are x + 2 and x + 4.

The sum of these three numbers is:
x + (x + 2) + (x + 4) = 246

Combine like terms:
3x + 6 = 246

Subtract 6 from both sides:
3x = 240

Divide by 3:
x = 80

Now we know the first even number is 80. The next two consecutive even numbers are 82 and 84.

The three consecutive even numbers are 80, 82, and 84. Answer: 80, 82, 84

B2-191

Problem. If there are four times as many apples as oranges in a certain fruit basket and the basket has 15 apples, how many fruits would Emiliano have consumed if he eats 2/3 of each fruit's quantity in the basket?
 First, we need to find out how many oranges there are in the basket. Since there are four times as many apples as oranges, and there are 15 apples, we can set up the equation:

Number of oranges = Number of apples / 4
Number of oranges = 15 apples / 4

Now, we calculate the number of oranges:

Number of oranges = 15 / 4
Number of oranges = 3.75

However, we cannot have a fraction of an orange in this context, so we need to adjust our approach. Since the question states "four times as many apples as oranges," it implies that the number of oranges must be a whole number. Therefore, we need to find a whole number solution for the number of oranges that keeps the ratio of apples to oranges at four times.

To maintain the 4:1 ratio, let's find the closest whole number of oranges that fits this condition. If we have 15 apples, we can divide those apples by 4 to find the closest number of oranges that maintains the ratio:

Number of oranges ≈ (Number of apples) / 4
Number of oranges ≈ 15 apples / 4
Number of oranges ≈ 3.75

Since we can't have a fraction of an orange, we round down to the nearest whole number to maintain the ratio as close as possible. Thus, we have:

Number of oranges = 3

Now, Emiliano eats 2/3 of each fruit's quantity. So, we need to calculate the amount of apples and oranges he would have consumed:

Consumed apples = (2/3) * Number of apples
Consumed apples = (2/3) * 15
Consumed apples = 10

Consumed oranges = (2/3) * Number of oranges
Consumed oranges = (2/3) * 3
Consumed oranges = 2

Finally, we add up the consumed quantities to find the total number of fruits Emiliano would have consumed:

Total consumed fruits = Consumed apples + Consumed oranges
Total consumed fruits = 10 + 2
Total consumed fruits = 12

Answer: 12

Problem. Amalia can read 4 pages of her book in 2 minutes. How many minutes will it take her to read 18 pages of her book?
 First, determine Amalia's reading rate in minutes per page:

Reading rate = (Time in minutes) / (Pages read)

Amalia reads 4 pages in 2 minutes, so her reading rate is:

Reading rate = 2 minutes / 4 pages = 1/2 minute per page

Now that we know Amalia takes 1/2 minute to read one page, we can find out the time it takes her to read 18 pages:

Time for 18 pages = Reading rate * Number of pages
Time for 18 pages = (1/2 minute/page) * 18 pages

To calculate the result, multiply:

Time for 18 pages = (1/2) * 18 = 9 minutes

So, it will take Amalia 9 minutes to read 18 pages of her book.

Answer: 9

Problem. A movie theater has 6 screens which show movies back-to-back all day. If the movie theater is open for many hours, and each movie lasts 2 hours, how many movies are shown in total throughout the day?
 To find the number of movies shown in total throughout the day, we need to determine how often the theater shows a movie and how long it takes until the next movie can start.

1. There are 6 screens in the theater, so 6 movies are shown simultaneously.

2. Each movie lasts 2 hours, which means that 2 hours pass before the next movie can start on any given screen.

3. Since there are 6 movies running in parallel, the entire theater utilizes the full 2 hours without any downtime.

4. If the theater is open for many hours, let's assume it's open for 12 hours (this is an arbitrary assumption to demonstrate the calculation, but the solution would be similar for a longer or shorter duration).

5. To find out how many times the theater can show a movie within these 12 hours, we divide the total open time (12 hours) by the duration of each movie (2 hours).

6. 12 hours / 2 hours = 6.

7. Therefore, the theater can show 6 batches of movies in 12 hours.

8. Since there are 6 movies being shown simultaneously, and 6 batches are shown in 12 hours, the total number of movies shown is 6 * 6 = 36.

Answer: 36.

Problem. Mary's sheep can run 12 feet per second, and her sheepdog can run 0 feet per second. A sheep standing 160 feet away from the sheep dog bolts, and the dog runs after it. How many seconds does it take the dog to catch the sheep?
 First, we need to determine the speed difference between the sheep and the sheepdog since the dog cannot move. 

Given:
- Sheep speed = 12 feet per second
- Sheepdog speed = 0 feet per second

First, we calculate the relative speed of the sheep in relation to the sheepdog.
Relative speed = Speed of sheep - Speed of sheepdog
Relative speed = 12 feet/second - 0 feet/second
Relative speed = 12 feet/second

Now, we can calculate the time it takes for the sheepdog to catch the sheep by dividing the distance between them by the relative speed.
Time = Distance / Relative speed
Time = 160 feet / 12 feet/second

The answer is:
Time = 13.33 seconds (rounded to two decimal places)

How much money does she earn if she sells all her milk and butter?

Since she wanted to turn all the leftover milk into butter and she sold enough milk for all her customers, she doesn't need to make any butter. So, the remaining money she made is from the milk sold alone. Since she made $144 from the milk sold, the final answer is $144.

B2-220

Problem. Layla is training for a bicycle race. She rode her bicycle slowly to the high school, rode four miles around the running track at her faster, race pace, and then rode back home, slowly, on the same route. How far is it, in miles, from Layla's house to the high school?
 
To find the distance from Layla's house to the high school, we need to analyze the information provided:

1. Layla rides her bicycle slowly to the high school. Let's call this distance 'x' miles.
2. She then rides four miles around the running track at her faster, race pace.
3. Finally, she rides back home, slowly, on the same route.

Since the route to the high school and back home is the same, the distance from Layla's house to the high school and back is twice 'x'. So the total distance Layla traveled in the round trip is x (to the high school) + 4 miles (on the running track) + x (back home).

The total distance Layla traveled is:
x + 4 + x = 2x + 4

Since we know that the total distance Layla traveled is 4 miles (the distance around the running track), we can set up the equation:
2x + 4 = 4

Now, let's solve for 'x':
2x = 0
x = 0

Therefore, the distance from Layla's house to the high school is 0 miles. 

Answer: 0

 First calculate the number of pencils Melissa wants by adding 1 to the number of pencils Tolu wants: 3 pencils + 1 = 4 pencils. Then calculate the total number of pencils the students want by adding the number Tolu wants to the number Robert wants to the number Melissa wants: 3 pencils + 4 pencils + 5 pencils = 12 pencils. B2-232

Problem. Francie saves up her allowance for several weeks. She receives an allowance of $5 a week for -8 weeks. Then her dad raises her allowance, and she receives -$6 a week for -6 weeks. Francie uses half of the money to buy new clothes. With the remaining money, she buys a video game that costs $35. How much money does Francie have remaining after buying the video game?
 First, we calculate the total amount Francie saves over the 8 weeks at $5 per week: $5 x 8 = $40. 

Next, we calculate her earnings over the next 6 weeks at $6 per week: $6 x 6 = $36. 

Now we add both amounts together to find her total savings: $40 + $36 = $76. 

Francie spends half of this money on new clothes, which is $76 / 2 = $38. 

After buying the clothes, she has $76 - $38 = $38 left. 

Finally, she spends $35 on a video game, leaving her with $38 - $35 = $3. 

Answer: 3

B2-233

Problem. Aren’s flight from New York to Hawaii will take 11 hours 20 minutes. He spends 2 hours reading, more than 4 hours watching two movies, less than 30 minutes eating his dinner, more than 40 minutes listening to the radio, and 1 hour 10 minutes playing games. How many hours does he have left to take a nap?
 First, let's convert all the activities to minutes to make the calculation easier.
- Reading: 2 hours = 120 minutes
- Watching movies: more than 4 hours = more than 240 minutes
- Eating dinner: less than 30 minutes = less than 30 minutes
- Listening to the radio: more than 40 minutes = more than 40 minutes
- Playing games: 1 hour 10 minutes = 70 minutes

Now let's add the time spent on these activities.

Total time spent = 120 + more than 240 + more than 40 + 70

Since we are asked for the exact time, and the other activities have more than time values, we'll consider the minimum total time for these activities, which is:

Minimum Total Time Spent = 120 + 240 + 40 + 70 = 470 minutes

Now let's convert the total flight time from hours to minutes:

Total flight time = 11 hours 20 minutes = 11 * 60 + 20 = 660 + 20 = 680 minutes

Now, let's find the time left for a nap:

Time left for nap = Total flight time - Minimum Total Time Spent = 680 - 470 = 210 minutes

Finally, let's convert the time left for a nap back to hours:

Time left for nap in hours = 210 minutes / 60 = 3.5 hours

So, Are has 3.5 hours left to take a nap.

Answer: 3.5

B2-241

Problem. Roberto is raising chickens for eggs. He buys 4 chickens for $20 each. They cost $1 in total a week to feed and each produces 3 eggs a week that will last forever. He used to buy many dozen eggs a week and spent $2 per dozen. After how many weeks will the chickens be cheaper than buying his eggs?
 First, calculate the initial cost of the chickens: Roberto buys 4 chickens at $20 each, so his initial cost is 4 * $20 = $80.

Next, let's find out how the cost of buying eggs compares to the cost of raising chickens. Roberto used to spend $2 per dozen eggs a week, and each chicken produces 3 eggs a week. So, one chicken produces enough eggs to replace one dozen eggs a week because 12 eggs / 3 eggs per chicken = 4 chickens. This means he could replace 4 chickens per week with eggs.

The weekly cost to feed the chickens is $1 per week. Now, let's compare the weekly cost of feeding the chickens ($1) to the weekly cost of buying eggs ($2) for 4 chickens. Every week, the savings by raising chickens instead of buying eggs is $2 - $1 = $1.

We need to find out how many weeks it will take for the total savings from raising chickens to cover the initial cost of $80. Since he saves $1 per week by raising chickens, we divide the initial cost by the weekly savings: $80 / $1 per week = 80 weeks.

Therefore, after 80 weeks, the chickens will be cheaper than buying his eggs.

Answer: 80

B2-246

Problem. One of the activities in Grade 6 PE class is to get the average weight of the students from each group. In one of the groups, the average weight of five girls is 45 kg while the average weight of five boys is 55 kg. What is the average weight of the seven students from that group?
 First, we need to find the total weight of the five girls and the five boys.

The total weight of the five girls = average weight × number of girls = 45 kg × 5 = 225 kg.
The total weight of the five boys = average weight × number of boys = 55 kg × 5 = 275 kg.

Now we add the total weight of the girls and the boys to get the combined total weight.
Combined total weight = weight of girls + weight of boys = 225 kg + 275 kg = 500 kg.

Since there are seven students in the group, we divide the combined total weight by the number of students to get the average weight.
Average weight of the seven students = combined total weight / number of students = 500 kg / 7 ≈ 71.43 kg.

Answer: 71.43

Problem. If there are four times as many apples as oranges in a certain fruit basket and the basket has 15 apples, how many fruits would Emiliano have consumed if he eats 2/3 of each fruit's quantity in the basket?

Oranges = Apples / 4
Oranges = 15 / 4
Oranges = 3.75

However, since we can't have a fraction of an orange in the basket, we'll assume that there are 3 oranges.

Now, let's find the total number of fruits in the basket:

Total fruits = Apples + Oranges
Total fruits = 15 + 3
Total fruits = 18

Apples consumed = (2/3) * Apples
Apples consumed = (2/3) * 15
Apples consumed = 10

Oranges consumed = (2/3) * Oranges
Oranges consumed = (2/3) * 3
Oranges consumed = 2

Now, let's find the total number of fruits Emiliano consumes:

Total fruits consumed = Apples consumed + Oranges consumed
Total fruits consumed = 10 + 2
Total fruits consumed = 12

Therefore, Emiliano would have consumed 12 fruits.

Answer the following question. Sam earns $10 an hour on Math tutoring. For the first month, he earned $200; and for the second month, he earned $150 more than the first month. How many hours did he spend on tutoring for two months?
To find the total number of hours Sam spent tutoring, we need to analyze the earnings over two months and determine how many hours correspond to those earnings.

1. **Earnings for the first month:**
   - Sam earned $200.
   - Since he earns $10 per hour, the number of hours he spent tutoring in the first month is:
     \[
     \frac{200}{10} = 20 \text{ hours}
     \]

2. **Earnings for the second month:**
   - Sam earned $150 more than the first month.
   - His earnings for the second month are:
     \[
     200 + 150 = 350 \text{ dollars}
     \]
   - Since he earns $10 per hour, the number of hours he spent tutoring in the second month is:
     \[
     \frac{350}{10} = 35 \text{ hours}
     \]

3. **Total hours spent tutoring over two months:**
   - Adding the hours from both months:
     \[
     20 + 35 = 55 \text{ hours}
     \]

Thus, the total number of hours Sam spent tutoring for two months is \boxed{55}.

B2-258

Problem. Joe likes to play video games and he normally spends $50 a month on video games. Joe also sells his games for $30 each once he is done playing them. If he starts with $240, how many months can he buy games at $50 and sell them at $30 before he is out of money?
eah bought 3 boxes of birdseed. When she went to put them away, she discovered that she already had 5 boxes in the pantry. Her parrot eats 100 grams of seeds each week and her cockatiel eats 50 grams. If each box of birdseed contains 225 grams, for how many weeks can she feed her birds without going back to the store?
Each birdseed box contains 225 grams. She has a total of 3 + 5 = 8 boxes. In total, she has 225 * 8 = 1800 grams. Her birds eat a total of 100 + 50 = 150 grams of seeds per week. Without going to the store, she can feed her birds for 1800 / 150 = 12 weeks.
Final Answer: 12

Let's think step by step. Mary spent $44 because .4 x 60 = 24. Mary had $60 before her purchase because 24 / .4 = 60. Mary spent $44 on pastry because 60 - 24 = 44. Each chocolate costs $4 because 2 + 4 - 2 = 4. Each pie costs $8 because $2 x 4 = $8. Mary bought 4 pies because 44 / 8 = 5.50. Solved it!

Answer:
First, let's find the total cost without the discount: $800 x 2 = $1600. Now, let's calculate the discount: $1600 x 5 / 100 = $80. Miley's final payment will be the total cost minus the discount: $1600 - $80 = $1520. So the answer is 1520.

 Let P be the number of push-ups Zachary did. Then David did P+27 push-ups. Let C be the number of crunches Zachary did. Then David did C-7. But we also know that C > P and P < 5. As the number of crunches you can do is an integer, let P = 4. This means that David did 4 + 27 = 31 push-ups. To maximize the number of crunches Zachary did compared to push-ups, we want to maximize C, so let C be as close as possible to P + 1. Thus C = 5. Problem. Lori owns a carsharing company. There are three red cars and two white cars available to rent. Renting the white car costs $2 for every minute and the red car $3 for every minute. All cars were rented for 3 hours. How much money did John earn?
 First, we need to calculate the earning from each car.

There are three red cars, each rented for 3 hours at $3 per minute.
Since there are 60 minutes in an hour, the total time the red cars were rented is 3 * 60 = 180 minutes.
The earning from one red car is 180 minutes * $3/minute = $540.
Since there are three red cars, the total earning from red cars is 3 * $540 = $1620.

There are two white cars, each rented for 3 hours at $2 per minute.
The total time the white cars were rented is 3 * 60 = 180 minutes.
The earning from one white car is 180 minutes * $2/minute = $360.
Since there are two white cars, the total earning from white cars is 2 * $360 = $720.

Now, to find the total earning, we add the earnings from red cars and white cars:
Total earning = Earning from red cars + Earning from white cars
Total earning = $1620 + $720 = $2340.

Answer: $2340

B2-280

Problem. Lucca has 100 balls and 10 percent of his balls are basketballs. Lucien has 200 balls and 20 percent of them are basketballs. In total how many footballs do Lucca and Lucien have?
 First, we need to find out how many basketballs each person has. For Lucca, 10% of his 100 balls are basketballs. So, Lucca has:

10% of 100 = 0.10 * 100 = 10 basketballs

For Lucien, 20% of his 200 balls are basketballs. So, Lucien has:

20% of 200 = 0.20 * 200 = 40 basketballs

Now, we want to find out how many footballs Lucca and Lucien have. Since Lucca and Lucien have 100 and 200 balls respectively, and we've already found out how many of those are basketballs, we can subtract the number of basketballs from the total number of balls to find the number of footballs:

For Lucca: 100 total balls - 10 basketballs = 90 footballs
For Lucien: 200 total balls - 40 basketballs = 160 footballs

Now, we add the number of footballs Lucca and Lucien have to find the total number of footballs:

90 footballs (Lucca) + 160 footballs (Lucien) = 250 footballs

So, in total, Lucca and Lucien have 250 footballs.

Answer: 250

B2-282

Problem. Luna's monthly food budget is equal to 60% of her house rental budget while her phone bill budget is 10% of her food budget. If the total budget of house rental and food budget is $240, how much is Luna's total monthly budget to pay all those expenses?
 First, we need to find Luna's house rental budget. Since the combined food and house rental budget is $240 and the food budget is 60% of the house rental budget, we can set up the following equation:

Let H be the house rental budget, then the food budget is 0.60H. So:
H + 0.60H = 240

Combining like terms gives us:
1.60H = 240

Divide both sides by 1.60 to find H:
H = 240 / 1.60
H = 150

Now that we have the house rental budget, we can find the food budget:
Food budget = 0.60 * H
Food budget = 0.60 * 150
Food budget = 90

Next, we need to find Luna's phone bill budget, which is 10% of her food budget:
Phone bill budget = 0.10 * Food budget
Phone bill budget = 0.10 * 90
Phone bill budget = 9

Finally, we need to find Luna's total monthly budget for all those expenses:
Total monthly budget = House Rental Budget + Food Budget + Phone Bill Budget
Total monthly budget = 150 + 90 + 9
Total monthly budget = 249

Answer: 249

However, the problem does not specify the total distance of the race, so we assume Suzanne runs for a total of `n` kilometers. 

1. In the first kilometer, her parents donate $10.
2. In the second kilometer, they donate $10 * 2 = $20.
3. In the third kilometer, they donate $20 * 2 = $40.
4. This pattern continues for the entire race.

To calculate the total donation, we need to sum up the donations for each kilometer. If she runs `n` kilometers, the total donation can be calculated using the formula for the sum of a geometric series:

Sum = a * ((r^n - 1) / (r - 1))

Where:
- `a` is the initial amount ($10),
- `r` is the common ratio (2, since the amount doubles each time),
- `n` is the number of terms (kilometers).

However, without the exact value of `n`, we cannot provide a specific total. If you can provide the total number of kilometers she runs, we can calculate the exact donation amount. Otherwise, the formula above shows how to compute it.

Problem. This morning, farmer Rita's workers collected 30,000 gallons of milk and chilled it in a storage tank. They then spent the next 4 hours pumping milk at a rate of 2,880 gallons/hour from the storage tank into a milk tanker. For the next 7 hours, the workers added more milk into the storage tank. How many gallons of milk were left in the storage tank?
 Step 1: Calculate the total gallons of milk pumped into the milk tanker.
The workers pumped milk at a rate of 2,880 gallons/hour for 4 hours. So, the total pumped milk is 2,880 gallons/hour * 4 hours = 11,520 gallons.

Step 2: Subtract the pumped milk from the total collected milk.
The workers collected 30,000 gallons of milk initially, and pumped out 11,520 gallons. So, milk left in storage after pumping is 30,000 - 11,520 = 18,480 gallons.

Step 3: Calculate the total added milk during the next 7 hours.
During the next 7 hours, the workers added more milk into the storage tank. The problem doesn't mention a specific rate, so we assume they added the same rate as the pumping: 2,880 gallons/hour. So, the added milk is 2,880 gallons/hour * 7 hours = 20,160 gallons.

Step 4: Add the added milk to the milk remaining in the storage tank.
After the pumping, 18,480 gallons were in the storage tank, and they added 20,160 gallons. So, the final amount of milk left in the storage tank is 18,480 + 20,160 = 38,640 gallons.

Step 5: Give the final answer.
Answer: 38,640

 First, we need to determine how much Joe actually gets for each game. Since he spends $50 and then sells the game for $30, he gets $30 - $50 = -$20 back each time.

Next, we need to figure out how many games will allow him to get back what he spends in a month. To break even (cover his monthly expenses), he would need enough money to buy 50 games when he got $30 each time, which means he needs $50 * (50/30) = $333.33.

Now, let's calculate how many months' worth of games he can buy with his initial $240:

$240 / $100 = 2.4

Since he can't buy a fraction of a game next month, we round down to 2 months.

So, Joe can buy games at $50 and sell them at $30 for 2 months before he is out of money.

1. Barry bought 12 pretzels.
2. Shelly bought half as many pretzels as Barry, so she bought 12 / 2 = 6 pretzels.
3. Angie bought x times as many pretzels as Shelly, so she bought 6 * x pretzels.

6 * x = 18

x = 18 / 6

x = 3

The value of the unknown variable x is 3.

Problem. Samuel has $5, $10, and $20 bills which amount to $150 in his wallet. His $10-bills amount to $50, and he has 4 $20-bills. How many bills does Samuel have in his wallet?

Problem. Charlotte needs to know how much money to have with her when she goes to the shoe store. How much money should Charlotte bring to buy a pair of boots, if there is a discount of 20%?
 To determine how much money Charlotte should bring, we need to know the original price of the pair of boots and the discount amount. Here's a step-by-step approach:

1. **Identify the original price**: Let's assume the original price of the pair of boots is \( P \).

2. **Calculate the discount amount**: The discount is 20%, so the discount amount is \( 0.20 \times P \).

3. **Determine the final price**: Subtract the discount amount from the original price to get the final price Charlotte needs to pay:
   \[
   \text{Final Price} = P - (0.20 \times P) = 0.80 \times P
   \]

4. **Calculate the amount to bring**: Charlotte should bring the final price, which is \( 0.80 \times P \).

Since the original price \( P \) is not provided in the problem, we can express the amount Charlotte should bring in terms of \( P \):

\[
\text{Amount to Bring} = 0.80 \times P
\]

**Final Answer**: 0.80P

Problem. Martha needs to paint all four walls in her 12 foot by 16 foot kitchen, which has 10 foot high ceilings. Each wall needs one coat of primer and two coats of paint. If Martha can paint 40 square feet per hour, how many hours will it take her to paint the kitchen?
 First, calculate the area of each wall:

- Two walls are 12 feet wide and 10 feet high. So, the area of each of these walls is 12 x 10 = 120 square feet.
- The other two walls are 16 feet wide and 10 feet high. So, the area of each of these walls is 16 x 10 = 160 square feet.

Martha needs to paint all four walls, so the total area to be painted is:
(2 x 120) + (2 x 160) = 240 + 320 = 560 square feet.

Since each wall needs one coat of primer and two coats of paint, Martha needs to paint 3 coats in total for each wall. Therefore, the total area to be painted is:
560 square feet x 3 coats = 1680 square feet.

Martha can paint 40 square feet per hour, so the number of hours it will take her to paint the kitchen is:
1680 square feet / 40 square feet per hour = 42 hours.

Answer: 42

12 cows * 4 gallons per cow = 48 gallons of milk

48 sticks of butter * $1.5 per stick = $72

Finally, let's add up her earnings from both milk and butter sales:
$72 from milk sales + $72 from butter sales = $144

Answer: 144

B2-365

Problem. Kim drives 30 miles to her friend's house. On the way back she has to take a detour that is 20% longer. She spends 30 minutes at her friend's house. She drives at a speed of 44 mph. How long did she spend away from home?
 

1. **Trip to the friend's house:**  
   Kim drives 30 miles to her friend's house.  
   Time taken = distance / speed = 30 miles / 44 mph ≈ 0.68 hours.

2. **Detour back home:**  
   The detour is 20% longer than the original distance.  
   Longer distance = 30 miles + (20% of 30 miles) = 30 + 6 = 36 miles.  
   Time taken = 36 miles / 44 mph ≈ 0.82 hours.

3. **Time spent at the friend's house:**  
   Kim spends 30 minutes here, which is 0.5 hours.

4. **Total time away from home:**  
   Total time = time to friend's house + detour time + time at friend's house  
   Total time = 0.68 + 0.82 + 0.5 ≈ 2.0 hours.

Final Answer: 2

B2-371

Problem. Hadley loves to do volunteer work at the local library. On a certain day, she neatly arranges 100 books on the shelf. By lunchtime, a certain number of books had been borrowed. She added 40 more books to the same shelf. By evening, 30 more books had been borrowed. If the shelf had 60 books remaining by the evening, how many books had been borrowed by lunchtime?
 Let X be the number of books borrowed by lunchtime. 100 books were on the shelf to start with, so X books were borrowed resulting in (100 - X) books. Hadley then added 40 books which resulted in X + 40 books on the shelf. 30 more books were borrowed by events, resulting in the (100 - X + 40 - 30) = 60 books that remained on the shelf. Adding 40 and subtracting 30 from 100 leads to 110. To find X, we subtract 110 from each side: X = 60 - 110. This leaves us with X = -50. The number of books borrowed by lunchtime is <number>: -50.

Problem. If Beth is 18 years old and her little sister is 5, in how many years would she be twice her sister's age?
 Let X be the number of years when Beth is twice her sister's age.
Beth's age in X years: 18 + X
Her sister's age in X years: 5 + X
We want to find when Beth's age is twice her sister's age, so we set up the equation 18 + X = 2(5 + X).
Solve for X: 18 + X = 10 + 2X
Subtract X from both sides: 18 = 10 + X
Subtract 10 from both sides: 8 = X
So, in 8 years, Beth will be twice her sister's age.
Answer: 8

Problem. Jerry’s two daughters play softball on different teams. They each have 8 games this season. Each team practices at most 10 hours for every game they play. If each game lasts for 1 or 2 hours, how many hours will Jerry spend at the field watching his daughters play and practice altogether?
 First, calculate the total number of games both daughters will play:

Two daughters, each with 8 games, so the total number of games is \(2 \times 8 = 16\) games.

Next, determine the maximum number of hours Jerry will spend at the field for the games:

Each game lasts either 1 or 2 hours, so let's assume the maximum time spent per game, which is 2 hours. Therefore, the total time for games is \(16 \times 2 = 32\) hours.

Now, calculate the maximum number of hours Jerry will spend at the field for the practices:

Each team practices at most 10 hours for every game, and there are 16 games in total. So, the total practice time is \(16 \times 10 = 160\) hours.

Finally, add the total time spent on games and practices:

\[
32 \text{ (hours for games)} + 160 \text{ (hours for practices)} = 192 \text{ hours}
\]

Therefore, Jerry will spend a total of 192 hours at the field watching his daughters play and practice altogether.

Answer: \boxed{192}

Problem. Francine wants to know if a local chemical plant is releasing toxic waste that makes the frogs mutate. In one local pond, she catches 5 frogs with extra legs, 2 frogs with 2 heads, two frogs that are bright red, and many normal frogs. What percentage of the frogs have mutated, rounded to the nearest integer?
 First, let's find the total number of frogs. 
- 5 frogs with extra legs
- 2 frogs with 2 heads
- 2 frogs that are bright red
- Many normal frogs (we can call this x)

Total number of frogs = 5 + 2 + 2 + x

Now, let's find the number of mutated frogs.
- Frogs with extra legs: 5
- Frogs with 2 heads: 2
- Frogs that are bright red: 2

Total mutated frogs = 5 + 2 + 2 = 9

Now, let's find the percentage of mutated frogs.
Percentage of mutated frogs = (Total mutated frogs / Total number of frogs) * 100

Percentage of mutated frogs = (9 / (5 + 2 + 2 + x)) * 100

However, we don't know the exact number of normal frogs (x). To calculate the approximate percentage, we can assume that "many normal frogs" is a significant number compared to the mutated frogs. For simplicity, let's consider x to be a large number, such as 10 times the number of mutated frogs, which is 9 * 10 = 90.

Now, let's recalculate the percentage using this assumption.

Total number of frogs = 5 + 2 + 2 + 90 = 99

Percentage of mutated frogs = (9 / 99) * 100 ≈ 9.09

Rounded to the nearest integer, the percentage of mutated frogs is 9.

Answer: 9

Problem. On a particular day, Rose's teacher read the register and realized there were twice as many girls as boys present on that day. The class has 250 students, and all the 140 girls were present. If all the absent students were boys, how many boys were absent that day?
 
First, we need to find out how many boys were present on that day. Since there were twice as many girls as boys present, and there are 140 girls present, we can set up the following equation:

Number of girls present = 2 * Number of boys present
140 = 2 * Number of boys present
Number of boys present = 140 / 2
Number of boys present = 70

Now we know that there are 70 boys present. Since the class has 250 students, we can find the number of boys present and absent:

Number of boys in the class = Number of boys present + Number of boys absent
250 - 140 (number of girls) = 70 (boys present) + Number of boys absent
110 (total number of boys in the class) = 70 (boys present) + Number of boys absent

Now, we can find the number of absent boys by subtracting the number of boys present from the total number of boys in the class:

Number of boys absent = 110 (total number of boys) - 70 (boys present)
Number of boys absent = 40

So, there were 40 boys absent on that day.

Answer: 40

Problem. 9 years from now, John will be -3 times as old as he was 11 years ago. How old is he now?
 Let J be John's friend's current age, in years. We know that, nine years from now, John will be J + 9 years old, and we know that 11 years ago, John will be J - 11 years old. 
Moreover, since he will be -3 times as old as he was 11 years ago, we can create an equation. 
-> J + 9 = -3(J - 11) 
-> J + 9 = -3J + 33 
-> J = -3J + 24 
Solving this equation by moving all terms involving J to the left side, we get: 
-> 4J = 24 
-> J = 6 
Thus, John is currently 6 years old.

Problem. Rebecca wants to split a collection of marbles into groups of 4. Rebecca has 10 eggs and more than 20 marbles. How many groups will be created?
 To determine how many groups of 4 marbles can be formed from Rebecca's collection, we need to consider the number of marbles she has. The problem states that Rebecca has more than 20 marbles but does not specify the exact number. However, since we need to form groups of 4, we can infer that the number of marbles must be a multiple of 4 to create complete groups.

Given that she has more than 20 marbles, the smallest possible number of marbles she could have is 21. But to form complete groups of 4, the number of marbles must be a multiple of 4. The next multiple of 4 after 20 is 24. Therefore, Rebecca must have at least 24 marbles to form complete groups of 4.

If Rebecca has 24 marbles, the number of groups she can create is calculated as follows:
\[ \text{Number of groups} = \frac{\text{Total number of marbles}}{\text{Number of marbles per group}} = \frac{24}{4} = 6 \]

So, Rebecca can create 6 groups of 4 marbles each.

Answer: 6

Show your reasoning briefly, then give the final numeric answer on the last line as 'Answer: <number>'.

Problem. 28 children were riding on the bus. At the bus stop 82 children got on the bus while some got off the bus. Then there were 30 children altogether on the bus..How many more children got on the bus than those that got off?
 First, we need to determine how many children got off the bus. We know that there were initially 28 children on the bus, and after some got on and some got off, there were 30 children on the bus.

So, the number of children who got on the bus minus the number of children who got off the bus should equal the final number of children minus the initial number of children:

82 (children who got on) - x (children who got off) = 30 (final number of children) - 28 (initial number of children)

Now, we solve for x, which represents the number of children who got off the bus:

82 - x = 30 - 28
82 - x = 2

Now, we isolate x:

x = 82 - 2
x = 80

Therefore, 80 children got off the bus.

Now we need to find out how many more children got on the bus than those that got off:

82 (children who got on) - 80 (children who got off) = 2

So, 2 more children got on the bus than those that got off.

Answer: 2

- Robert wants 5 pencils, so he will spend 5 * $0.20 = $1.00.
- Melissa wants M pencils, so she will spend M * $0.20.

Total cost = $0.60 + $1.00 + (M * $0.20)

 There are three times 1 = 3 times more trees than stones in the courtyard. The number of trees is 3 times the number of stones in the yard, a total of 3 + 1 = 4 times the number of stones. There are twice 4 = 8 times as many birds as the number of trees and stones in the yard. The trees have 8 times the number of birds in the yard, a total of 1 + 3 = 4 times the number of birds. The number of birds in the trees is 2 birds/stone * 4 times = 8 birds. Answer: 8.

Joe can buy games and sell them for 12 months before he runs out of money.

B2-496

Problem. Ed had more marbles than Doug. Doug lost 11 of his marbles at the playground. If Ed had 45 marbles.How many more marbles did Ed have than Doug then?
 
First, we need to determine how many marbles Doug had after losing 11 marbles. Since Doug lost 11 marbles, we can subtract that from the original number of marbles Doug had. However, we don't know how many marbles Doug originally had, but we know that Ed had more marbles than Doug.

If Ed had 45 marbles and Doug lost 11 marbles, we need to find out how many more marbles Ed has than Doug after Doug lost the marbles. Since we don't know Doug's original number of marbles, we can only calculate the difference between Ed's marbles and the number of marbles Doug had before losing any, which we'll call D.

After Doug lost 11 marbles, he would have D - 11 marbles left.

To find out how many more marbles Ed had than Doug after the loss, we subtract Doug's remaining marbles from Ed's marbles:

Ed's marbles - Doug's remaining marbles = 45 - (D - 11)

Now, we can simplify this expression:

45 - D + 11 = 45 - D + 11 = 56 - D

Since we don't have D, the original number of marbles Doug had, we can't calculate the exact number of more marbles Ed had than Doug after the loss. But we know that Ed had 56 more marbles than Doug before Doug lost any, and after losing 11 marbles, Doug would have 11 fewer marbles than he did originally.

So, the answer is that Ed had 56 - 11 = 45 more marbles than Doug after the loss. 

Therefore, 'Answer: 45'.

B2-498

Problem. Aren’s flight from New York to Hawaii will take 11 hours 20 minutes. He spends 2 hours reading, more than 4 hours watching two movies, less than 30 minutes eating his dinner, more than 40 minutes listening to the radio, and 1 hour 10 minutes playing games. How many hours does he have left to take a nap?
 
First, we need to convert all time spent into minutes for easier calculation. 

Flight duration:
11 hours 20 minutes = (11 * 60) + 20 = 660 + 20 = 680 minutes

Reading: 2 hours = 2 * 60 = 120 minutes
Watching movies: more than 4 hours = more than (4 * 60) = more than 240 minutes
Eating dinner: less than 30 minutes
Listening to the radio: more than 40 minutes
Playing games: 1 hour 10 minutes = (1 * 60) + 10 = 60 + 10 = 70 minutes

Total time spent:

The question does not specify exactly how much time Aren spends watching movies or listening to the radio, so we will use the inequalities provided to find the minimum and maximum possible time he has left.

Minimum time spent:
Reading: 120 minutes
Watching movies: 240 minutes
Eating dinner: 30 minutes (using the "less than" condition, we use the smallest possible integer value)
Listening to the radio: 40 minutes
Playing games: 70 minutes

Total minimum time spent:
120 + 240 + 30 + 40 + 70 = 500 minutes

Maximum time spent:
Reading: 120 minutes
Watching movies: more than 240 minutes (for the purpose of finding the maximum time for napping, we'll use 240 minutes)
Eating dinner: 30 minutes
Listening to the radio: 40 minutes
Playing games: 70 minutes

Total maximum time spent:
120 + 240 + 30 + 40 + 70 = 500 minutes

Since both minimum and maximum time spent are the same, Aren will spend a total of 500 minutes on his activities. 

To find the time left for napping:
Flight duration - Total time spent
680 minutes - 500 minutes = 180 minutes

Convert the time left for napping to hours:
180 minutes = 180 / 60 = 3 hours

Answer: 3

5 boys and 5 girls are standing in a row. How many ways are there for them to stand such that no girl stands next to another girl?
Solve the problem. Show your reasoning briefly, then give the final numeric answer on the last line as 'Answer: <number>'.
Answer: Let's think step by step. If no girl stands next to another girl, then there will be no two adjacent girls or boy. We separate each boy by a girl from the first 5 to the last 5. There will be 5 missing girls between them. Now we find a way to arrange (without the same is taken 2 girls) the 5 remaining girls and 5 boys, which is 5! * 5! = 14400..
The answer is 14400.

Bill has played 200 games of poker and won 63% of them. If he plays 100 more games and loses 43 of them, what is his new win percentage?

Solve the problem. Show your reasoning briefly, then give the final numeric answer on the last line as 'Answer: <number>'.
Answer: Let's think step by step. Bill has already won 63% of 200 games, which is 0.63 * 200 = 126 games. He plays 100 more games and loses 43, so he wins 100 - 43 = 57 games. His new total number of games is 200 + 100 = 300, and the total number of games won is 126 + 57 = 183. To find the new win percentage, we divide the number of games won by the total number of games played and multiply by 100: (183 / 300) * 100 = 61%. The answer is 61.

Problem. Jean needs to buy 10 new pairs of pants. A store is running a sale for 20% off. How much will she need to pay for all of them after paying a 10% tax?
 First, let's calculate the discounted price for one pair of pants. If there's a 20% discount, Jean only pays 80% of the original price. Next, we'll find out how much she needs to pay after including a 10% tax on the total discounted amount before tax. To do this, we first need to know the original price per pair of pants, let's call it P. Without knowing P, we can only express the final amount Jean pays in terms of P. However, if we assume P is some value, we can demonstrate the method.

Let's denote the original price for one pair of pants as P.

1. Calculate the discounted price per pair of pants: \( P \times 0.8 \).
2. Calculate the total price for 10 pairs of pants at the discounted rate: \( 10 \times (P \times 0.8) = 8P \).
3. Calculate the tax (10%) on the total discounted price: \( 8P \times 0.1 = 0.8P \).
4. Add the tax to the total discounted price: \( 8P + 0.8P = 8.8P \).

So, after all discounts and taxes, Jean will need to pay \( 8.8P \) for 10 pairs of pants.

Answer: \( 8.8P \)

Problem. Maddy is in college for 8 semesters. She needs 120 credits to graduate. If each class is 2 or 3 or 4 credits, how many classes does she need to take per semester?
 First, determine the minimum and maximum number of classes Maddy could take per semester.

If Maddy takes only 4-credit classes, then she could take:
120 credits / 4 credits per class = 30 classes over 8 semesters 
30 classes / 8 semesters = 3.75 classes per semester, which rounds up to 4 classes per semester.

If Maddy takes only 2-credit classes, she would need:
120 credits / 2 credits per class = 60 classes over 8 semesters 
60 classes / 8 semesters = 7.5 classes per semester, which rounds up to 8 classes per semester.

Since any combination of 2-, 3-, and 4-credit classes will not affect the minimum and maximum, Maddy could potentially take anywhere from 4 to 8 classes per semester depending on the credit distribution of her classes.

To get the final numeric answer, we need to consider the most consistent scenario:

Answer: 4

 will spend / watch at least 8 (games) x 2 (hours) = 16 hours in the field. Since Jerry has two daughters, this means 2 (daughters) x 16 (hours) = 32 hours at the field watching them play. Problem. It’s February 2021. Graham is 3 years younger than Mark, and Graham’s sister, Janice, is 1/2 the age of Graham. How old is Janice?

Process:  Food that is ingested is then taken to the stomach where it is broken down by stomach acids.  Food in the stomach is broken down into a soupy mass that is churned and mixed.  After the food has been broken down in the stomach, it is then moved to the small intestine where digestion is further carried out, and nutrients are absorbed in to the body.  There is an enzyme called amylase in saliva that breaks down carbohydrates.
This justifies what answer for what question? Q & A: What are the steps involved in the digestion process of food in the human body?

The digestion process of food in the human body occurs in several steps:

1. Ingestion: Food is taken into the body through the mouth.
2. Mastication (chewing): Food is mechanically broken down by the teeth.
3. Saliva Production: Saliva is produced in the salivary glands, which contains the enzyme amylase that begins the chemical breakdown of carbohydrates.
4. Swallowing: The food, now in smaller particles, is swallowed and moves down the esophagus with the help of peristaltic contractions.

Now, let's address the question: How old is Janice in February 2021?

Problem. One of the activities in Grade 6 PE class is to get the average weight of the students from each group. In one of the groups, the average weight of five girls is 45 kg while the average weight of five boys is 55 kg. What is the average weight of the seven students from that group?
 There are five girls with an average weight of 45 kg, so the total weight of the girls is 45 kg / girl x 5 girls = 225 kg. There are also five boys with an average weight of 55 kg, so the total weight of the boys is 55 kg / boy x 5 boys = 275 kg. The total weight of all seven students is 225 kg + 275 kg = 500 kg. To find the average weight of the seven students, we divide the total weight by the number of students: 500 kg / 7 students = 71.43 kg / student. Therefore, the average weight of the seven students is 71.43 kg.
Answer: 71.43

Labor cost per day = Hourly rate * Hours per day
Labor cost per day = $60/hour * 8 hours/day
Labor cost per day = $480/day

Now, let's calculate the total labor cost for the entire duration of the work. We don't have the exact number of days, but we can express the total cost as a function of the number of days. Let's call the number of days "d." Then, the total labor cost is:

Total labor cost = Labor cost per day * Number of days
Total labor cost = $480/day * d days

Next, let's add the cost of parts to find the total cost for the car's owner:

Total cost = Total labor cost + Cost of parts
Total cost = ($480/day * d days) + $2500

Problem. Martha needs to paint all four walls in her 12 foot by 16 foot kitchen, which has 10 foot high ceilings. Each wall needs one coat of primer and two coats of paint. If Martha can paint 40 square feet per hour, how many hours will it take her to paint the kitchen?
 Martha has a kitchen with two adjacent 12ft x 10ft walls and two adjacent 16ft x 10ft walls. Each wall needs to be painted twice, and Martha can paint 40 square feet per hour. The time it takes her is 2 x (12ft * 10ft + 16ft * 10ft) / 40 square feet per hour = 2 x (120ft2 + 160ft2) / 40 square feet per hour = 2 x 280ft2 / 40 square feet per hour = 560ft2 / 40 square feet per hour = 14 hours. B2-525

Problem. Mary is counting the number of minnows in the pond. 40% of the minnows have red bellies, 30% have green bellies and the rest have white or black bellies. If 20 minnows have red bellies, how many minnows have black bellies?
 
First, we need to find the total number of minnows in the pond. We know that 40% of the minnows have red bellies and that there are 20 minnows with red bellies. So, we can calculate the total number of minnows by using the following equation:

20 minnows * (100% / 40%) = Total number of minnows

To simplify this, we can divide both sides by 20 and multiply both sides by 40 to get:

100 * (100/40) = Total number of minnows

This simplifies to:

250 = Total number of minnows

Now we know that there are 250 minnows in total. Next, let's find out how many minnows have black bellies. We know that 40% of the minnows have red bellies, 30% have green bellies, and the rest have white or black bellies. So, we can subtract the percentages of red and green bellies from 100% to find the percentage of minnows with white or black bellies:

100% - 40% - 30% = 30%

This means that 30% of the minnows have white or black bellies. Now, we can calculate how many minnows have black bellies by using the following equation:

30% of 250 minnows = Number of minnows with black bellies

To simplify this, we can multiply both sides by 0.3 to get:

0.3 * 250 = Number of minnows with black bellies

This simplifies to:

75 = Number of minnows with black bellies

Therefore, 75 minnows have black bellies. Answer: 75

B2-531

Problem. Bobby had 21 pieces of candy. He ate more than 5 pieces of candy. Then he ate 9 more. How many pieces of candy does he still have left?
 To find out how many pieces of candy Bobby still has left, we need to follow these steps:

1. Start with the initial amount of candy Bobby had: 21 pieces.
2. Since Bobby ate more than 5 pieces of candy, let's assume he ate exactly 5 pieces (since it's the minimum): 21 - 5 = 16 pieces remaining.
3. Then he ate 9 more pieces: 16 - 9 = 7 pieces remaining.

So, Bobby still has 7 pieces of candy left. 

Here is the Python code to verify this reasoning:

```python
# Initial amount of candy Bobby had
initial_candy = 21

# Assuming Bobby ate exactly 5 pieces of candy first
first_eaten = 5
remaining_after_first_eaten = initial_candy - first_eaten

# Then he ate 9 more pieces of candy
second_eaten = 9
remaining_after_second_eaten = remaining_after_first_eaten - second_eaten

# Final answer
print(remaining_after_second_eaten)
```
```output
7
```
The final answer is \(\boxed{7}\).

Problem. William is a jockey. He can ride his horse for at most 6 hours a day. Over 6 days, he only used the maximum riding time twice. On two days he rode his horse for only several hours a day and half the maximum time for the next two days. How many hours did William ride his horse during those 6 days?
 There are six days, so the total maximum possible time, if he's ridden the horse for a maximum of 6 hours a day, is 6 * 6 = 36 hours. Over the 6 days he only used the maximum riding time twice, so he rode at maximum for a total of 6 * 2 = 12 hours. On two days he only rode hours a day, so he rode a total for 6 * 2 = 12 hours. For the next two days he rode the horse for half the maximum recommended time, so during those two days he rode for 3 * 2 = 6 hours. So far he has thus ridden for 12 + 12 + 6 = 30 hours. The total maximum possible time, minus the time ridden over 6 days is 36 - 30 = 6. Answer: 6.

Problem. The Razorback shirt shop makes $ 98 dollars off each shirt sold. During the Arkansas game and the Texas tech game they sold a total of 163 shirts. If they sold more than 89 shirts during the Arkansas game.How much money did they make from selling the shirts during the arkansas game?
 First, let's find out how many shirts were sold during the Texas Tech game. We know that a total of 163 shirts were sold during both games combined. Let's denote the number of shirts sold during the Arkansas game as A and the number of shirts sold during the Texas Tech game as T. We have:

A + T = 163

Now, we are given that they sold more than 89 shirts during the Arkansas game. Let's say they sold at least 90 shirts during the Arkansas game. So, we can write:

A ≥ 90

Now, we want to find out how many shirts were sold during the Arkansas game. Since we are only given information about a total and the condition of selling more than 89 shirts during the Arkansas game, we cannot determine the exact number of shirts sold during the Arkansas game. However, we can calculate the minimum amount of money they made from selling shirts during the Arkansas game.

To find the minimum amount of money, we assume they sold exactly 90 shirts during the Arkansas game (since this is the smallest whole number greater than 89). The total revenue from selling 90 shirts at $98 each would be:

Revenue = Number of shirts * Price per shirt
Revenue = 90 * $98
Revenue = $8,820

So, they made at least $8,820 from selling shirts during the Arkansas game. 

Answer: $8,820
