 In the second week, the store sold 15 x 3 = 45 bags of chips. There were 15 + 45 = 60 bags of chips sold in the first and second week. Therefore, 100 - 60 = 40 bags of chips were sold in the third and fourth week. Thus, the store sold 40/2 = 20 bags of chips in the third and fourth week each.

12 hours (day 1 and 2) + 6 hours (day 3 and 4) = 18 hours

Since we cannot determine the exact number of hours for the last two days, our final answer based on the information given is:

Problem. Jonas is a book collector. He has so many books he is converting his third bedroom into a library. He plans to put several bookshelves in the room and each bookshelf takes up 80 square feet of space. If he reserves 160 square feet of space to use for a desk and some walking space, how many shelves can he put in the room?
 First, we need to determine the total amount of space Jonas has available for bookshelves. We know that the room is 320 square feet in total, and he reserves 160 square feet for a desk and walking space. To find out how much space is left for bookshelves, we subtract the reserved space from the total space:

320 - 160 = 160 square feet

Now, we need to find out how many bookshelves can fit in the remaining space. Each bookshelf takes up 80 square feet, so we divide the remaining space by the space each bookshelf takes up:

160 / 80 = 2

Jonas can put 2 bookshelves in the room.

Answer: 2

Problem. If John travels 0 miles on a bike ride, and Jill travels 5 miles less, how many miles does Jim travel if he travels only 20% as far as Jill?
 First, we need to determine how far Jill travels. Since John travels 0 miles and Jill travels 5 miles less than John, Jill travels:
0 - 5 = -5 miles

However, it is not possible to travel negative miles in this context, so let's assume the problem meant that Jill travels 5 miles less than a positive distance. Let's assume John travels 5 miles instead (the negative value doesn't make sense in this scenario).

So, Jill travels:
5 - 5 = 0 miles

Now, we need to find out how far Jim travels. Jim travels only 20% as far as Jill, so:
20% of 0 miles = 0.20 * 0 miles = 0 miles

Answer: 0

 Zachary did 5 less push-ups, meaning Zachary did 5 + 27 = 32 push-ups. Let x be the number of crunches Zachary did. X - 7 > x. Solving the inequality, x > 7 + x. We know that x is greater than 7, but less than 5. There is no number X such that this inequality is true.

Problem. Roe saved $100 per month from January to July and saved $15 per month from August to November. How much should she save in December so she will have a total savings of $150 in the year?
 In January to July, she saved 100 x 7 = 700 dollars. From August to November, she saved $15 x 4 = $60. Her total savings from January to November is 700 + 60 = 760 dollars. To have a total savings of 150 dollars in the year, she needs to save 150 - 760 = -610 dollars in December. Therefore, Answer: -610.

B2-592

Problem. Jean needs to buy 10 new pairs of pants. A store is running a sale for 20% off. How much will she need to pay for all of them after paying a 10% tax?
 First, we need to determine how much Jean will spend on the 10 pairs of pants after the 20% discount.

If Jean buys 10 pairs of pants at the full price, let's denote the original price of one pair of pants as P. After a 20% discount, the new price for one pair of pants would be:

Price after discount = P - (20% of P) = P - 0.2P = 0.8P

So, for 10 pairs of pants with the discount, the total cost would be:

Total cost after discount = 10 * 0.8P = 8P

Next, we need to calculate the tax on this discounted total cost. Jean needs to pay a 10% tax on the total cost of the pants:

Tax amount = 10% of (8P) = 0.1 * 8P = 0.8P

Finally, to find out how much Jean will need to pay after including the tax, we add the tax amount to the total cost after the discount:

Total cost after tax = Total cost after discount + Tax amount = 8P + 0.8P = 8.8P

Therefore, Jean will need to pay a total of 8.8 times the original price of one pair of pants after all discounts and taxes are applied.

Answer: 8.8 * P

B2-593

Problem. The number of trees is three times more than the number of stones in the courtyard. There are also twice as many birds in the trees as the combined number of trees and stones in the yard. How many birds are in the trees?
 To solve the problem, we will follow these steps:

1. Determine the relationship between the number of trees and the number of stones.
2. Determine the relationship between the number of birds and the combined number of trees and stones.
3. Calculate the number of birds in the trees based on the relationships found in steps 1 and 2.

Step 1: Let's denote the number of stones as S and the number of trees as T. The problem states that the number of trees is three times more than the number of stones, so we can write this as T = 3S.

Step 2: The problem also states that there are twice as many birds in the trees as the combined number of trees and stones. Let's denote the number of birds as B. So, we can write this as B = 2(T + S).

Step 3: Now, we need to substitute the value of T from step 1 into the equation from step 2:

B = 2(3S + S)
B = 2(4S)
B = 8S

Since we don't have the exact number of stones (S), we cannot find the exact number of birds (B). However, we can express the number of birds in terms of the number of stones. So, the number of birds in the trees is 8 times the number of stones.

Answer: <unknown>

B2-595

Problem. 9 years from now, John will be -3 times as old as he was 11 years ago. How old is he now?
 Let \( x \) be John's current age. 

In 9 years, his age will be \( x + 9 \). 

Eleven years ago, his age was \( x - 11 \). 

According to the problem, in 9 years, he will be -3 times as old as he was 11 years ago, so we can write the equation: 

\( x + 9 = -3(x - 11) \) 

Now, let's solve for \( x \): 

\( x + 9 = -3x + 33 \) 
\( x + 3x = 33 - 9 \) 
\( 4x = 24 \) 
\( x = \frac{24}{4} \) 
\( x = 6 \) 

So, John is currently 6 years old. 

Answer: 6

Problem. Wynter went to her local town bike shop to buy her sister a bicycle as her birthday gift. While at the shop, Wynter counted many bicycles and many tricycles. How many wheels in total did the vehicles she saw have?
 There are two types of vehicles: bicycles with 2 wheels each and tricycles with 3 wheels each. We need to find the total number of wheels.

Let B represent the number of bicycles and T represent the number of tricycles.

The total number of wheels (W) can be calculated as follows:
W = (Number of wheels per bicycle * Number of bicycles) + (Number of wheels per tricycle * Number of tricycles)
W = (2 * B) + (3 * T)

However, we do not have specific values for B or T; thus, we cannot calculate a single numeric value for W without those details. To get an exact answer, additional information about the count of bicycles and tricycles would be needed.

Problem. Joe likes to play video games and he normally spends $50 a month on video games. Joe also sells his games for $30 each once he is done playing them. If he starts with $240, how many months can he buy games at $50 and sell them at $30 before he is out of money?
 Joe buys games for $50 and sells them for $30, so he loses $20 a month. The net purchase cost for a game is $50. The net loss for 5 full months is $100. Joe starts out with $240, so he will have $140 (14 games worth) after 5 months. At 5 months out of money, Joe can use this amount to buy 7 more games before buying another at a loss. For these 7 months, he loses $140, or in total $10. Adding the 2 games he bought in the earlier 5 months, Joe can buy 9 full games in a series of 12 months before running out of money. Answer: 12

B2-598

Problem. There are twice as many cows in Devonshire as there are hearts on a standard deck of 52 playing cards. If there are 4 hearts on a card, calculate the total cost of the cows when they are sold at $200 each.
 One heart card has 4 hearts, and thus the total number of hearts in the deck is 4 * 1 = 4 hearts per card. When there are 52 cards in the deck, the total number of hearts on a standard deck is 4 hearts * 52 cards = 208 hearts in the deck. The cows in Devonshire are twice as many as the hearts in a deck of cards, giving a total of 2 * 208 hearts = 416 cows in Devonshire. When sold at $200 each, 416 cows will have a total cost of $200 * 416 cows = $83200. There are 416 cows in Devonshire. Answer: 416.

B2-599

Problem. Two teams are playing in a stadium with a capacity of 2000 people. The total number of people in the stadium is 3/4 of the total capacity and each person paid differently in entry fees. What is the difference between the total amount of fees collected when the stadium was 3/4 full and if the stadium would have been full?
 The total number of people in the stadium is / 3 / 4 * 2000 = 1500. The revenue when the stadium is 3 / 4 full is 20 * 1500 = $30000. If the stadium were full, the total amount of fees collected would have been 40 * 2000 = $80000. Therefore, the difference in the total amount of fees collected between the stadium being 3 / 4 full and full is 80000 - 30000 = 50000.

Answer: 50000

B2-600

Problem. Hadley loves to do volunteer work at the local library. On a certain day, she neatly arranges 100 books on the shelf. By lunchtime, a certain number of books had been borrowed. She added 40 more books to the same shelf. By evening, 30 more books had been borrowed. If the shelf had 60 books remaining by the evening, how many books had been borrowed by lunchtime?
 Let X be the number of books borrowed by lunchtime. Hadley arranged 100 books, and X books were borrowed, so there were 100 - X books left. She added 40 more books, making it 100 - X + 40 books. By evening, 30 more books were borrowed, leaving 100 - X + 40 - 30 = 60 books. This simplifies to 110 - X = 60. Solving for X, we find X = 50.
Answer: 50
